ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u4	Dear President, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! On 14 October 2020, the Group of 5 thousand voters and voters submitted to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia, pursuant to Article 88 of the Constitution of Slovenia and Article 114 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, for consideration and adoption of the Proposal for a Law amending and supplementing the Postal Services Act. As indicated in the proposal paper, the aim of the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Postal Service Act is to ensure a consistent transposition of Directive 97/67 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 December 1997 on common rules for the development of the internal market for postal services in the Community and for the improvement of the quality of service. Let us point out that Slovenia has prescribed one of the highest quality of universal postal service in the European Union, since the Post Office of Slovenia must carry at least 95% of mail correspondence in one day, at least 99.5% of mail correspondence in two working days and 100% of mail correspondence in three working days. At the same time, the price of these services is regulated and is among the lowest in the European Union in Slovenia. It is also stated that due to the very high standards for the implementation of the universal postal service, regulated prices and the fact that the classical market for written services is also declining due to the Rural Development Programme for the construction of broadband networks with the aim of digitising the company, in particular in 2007-2015, and this was also allocated to EUR 86.3 million of resources and resources from the budget of the Republic of Slovenia, and consequently to the reduction of the use of classical written services. Of course, it is expected that the universal postal service provider will record the net cost as an unjustified financial burden in the future, so it is proposed to amend the Postal Services Act. In addition, it is proposed that the second paragraph of Article 19 be supplemented in order to determine when the net cost of universal service obligations is an unfair burden if the revenue from universal service provision does not fully cover the cost of universal service provision, including the cost of maintaining the postal network. We can also note that, for the first time in 2016, the Slovenian Post Office calculated this cost of EUR 17.4 million, but the Agency for Communications Networks and Services of the Republic of Slovenia (AKOS) did not recognise this cost after the examination in accordance with the prescribed methodology. The post office also recalculated the net cost of EUR 32 million for 2017, which was also not met by AKOS. On 13. 12. has issued a decision rejecting the request for payment since, after careful and thorough examination of all relevant circumstances on the basis of the applicable rules, it has not been established that the Postal Service of Slovenia, by implementing the obligation of the universal postal service, generates a net cost as an unfair burden. [[Incomprehensible]] The post office also calculated a net cost of almost EUR 37 million for 2018. The request was also reviewed by AKOS. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group, we are aware of these losses that the Post Office has with universal postal service. We are also aware that we must also prevent the decline and, above all, the closure of rural post offices. We believe that there is more than one solution. As the first solution, there is a change in the strategy for the management of state property, in particular in this direction that the profitability of Post Slovenia is reduced and that Mail Slovenia is recognised as an important institution, especially as an important institution in many remote villages, in rural areas. Last but not least, respected colleagues, Mail does not only carry out package services or universal postal services, the Post Office of Slovenia also has the authority of Banka Slovenije, in the countryside many pensioners can raise money through Post Slovenia, pay bills and so on. I do not think it is possible to expect digitalisation in 2020 or in the coming years. In the SDS Group, we will reject the bill, and we are looking for a solution together in the coalition to reduce the closure of rural businesses, and in particular we are looking for a solution in the State Property Management Strategy in the Republic of Slovenia where Post would be exempted. Mail as an institution must primarily pursue public good and its services. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u6	Dear President, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! In recent years there has been much talk about the problem of closing post offices and the decline in demand for universal postal services. We have pointed out several times in the LMŠ Group that maintaining the rural postal network for Slovenia is of great importance, especially in terms of social criteria and providing appropriate infrastructure in the countryside. Even the current exceptional growth in the number of packages does not weigh down the revenues recorded in the Post Office of Slovenia precisely because they provide universal postal services. The negative result, which creates an unsustainable situation in our national post office, has been a good time in the management and also in the Postal Workers Union, and in one voice they call for the country to listen and approach to solving the problem. To this end, the Postal Workers' Union has drawn up a legislative proposal amending and supplementing the Postal Services Act, which provides that the State will financially support the implementation of the universal postal service and thus prevent further closure of post offices and the decline in the quality of universal postal service. The amendment of the law would ensure that compensation for the obligation to provide universal service is granted in accordance with the State aid rules when it constitutes a net cost of undue financial burden. In a good month, the proposed changes supported more than 7,000 voters, who also put the law into parliamentary treatment. To compare, we can make arrangements in other European countries where some countries have already listened to calls and co-finance universal postal services from budgetary resources; again, under European law, there are specific funds for co-financing this service. Users rightly want such a postal service that will maintain the current level of service and maintain the existing network of businesses. We are aware that all technological changes and the rapid development of business digitisation will not be sustainable and the state must maintain a certain measure of classical postal activities and also promote socially responsible transitions to new forms of business, which will still allow access to public services for all citizens regardless of age group or residence. One of the most important roles in maintaining rural settlement is certainly also access to essential local services, i.e. postal services, regardless of how profitable they are. Universal postal services have been declining due to e-substitution and changing user habits for several years and the epidemic has accelerated this trend. In the second wave of the epidemic, the biggest decline is recorded in the field of direct mail and additional measures are expected to further decline in other groups of written consignments. Perhaps now was the time to listen to the bill and approach the problem; but unfortunately we missed the opportunity because the law on the committee did not receive sufficient support.

The postal service of Slovenia has thus become a provider of the so-called universal postal service, which imposes the transfer of all small postal shipments at a single price. The Post Office State covers a certain price in the law, taking into account that Slovenian regulations provide for a relatively dense network of postal shops and that trends in postal shipments are moving in the direction of an increasing number of packages and an increasing number of written shipments. Therefore, the post office must perform the postal service at the quality and price determined by the State, in all other cases it is a normal company from which the owner – again a country – expects in particular profit. In such circumstances, it is not surprising that the Post is trying to obtain additional revenue with additional services and to reduce costs also at the expense of reducing the network of post offices and excessive burdens on workers. It's not surprising, but it's sad. The plan Post Slovenia foresees almost half the reduction in the number of post offices. Your Honor, this was all a problem before the outbreak occurred. ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u10	Good morning. In the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, the proposed amendments to the Postal Services Act cannot be accepted because there are no objective reasons to change the way in which the possible net costs of the universal postal service obligation can be financed. The solutions proposed cannot be supported because of the non-compliance with the prescribed methodology prescribed by the Postal Services Act and the General Act on Accounting Information and the calculation of the net cost of universal postal service obligations. Following the examination of all relevant circumstances, it was not found that the Post of Slovenia by implementing the universal postal service obligation creates a net cost as an unfair financial burden. Slovenia’s post office has already prepared calculations of the net cost of 2016, 2017 and 2018 three times. These calculations ranged from €17.4 million to €36.9 million per year. In accordance with the prescribed methodology, the Agency for Communications Networks and Services (ACOS) has rejected all existing claims for payment of the net cost on the basis of the Postal Service Act and the general act already mentioned. Common sense also finds it difficult to explain the reason why public money would add to the service that some of its services charge at incomprehensible retail rates. At a potential recognised net cost as an unjustified financial burden for the universal postal service provider, we will address a proposal to the Government of the Republic of Slovenia to examine various ways of financing the implementation of UPS. In doing so, we believe that it will have to pay particular attention in particular to meeting the reasonable needs of the users of this service and to ensuring their quality. One must hold – users must be provided with a quality universal postal service, as prescribed by law. In the Republic of Slovenia, a specific compensation fund is in line with the European Post Service Directive as a financing mechanism for the net cost of UPS; however, the conditions for its establishment have not been met so far, and therefore not any circumstances and reasons that would require a change in the way in which the possible net costs of the obligation to implement UPS are financed. While in Slovenia, according to other European countries, high quality implementation of UPS is prescribed, modern trends in the postal services market, in addition to moving to e-substitution, promote liberalisation and competition in a way that reduces the scope and regulation of UPS and reduces the burden. In view of the increasingly changing situation in the postal services market, it is necessary to adapt to the simultaneous needs of users. E-mail has significantly reduced the number of mail transfers, and postal business visits are also declining. This is a general trend at European Union level and therefore one of the challenges of UPS operators in all European Union countries is to adapt the network to the changing needs of users. Due to the continuous decline in classical services provided by residents and residents in postal windows, the postal network is becoming less profitable, but it should be stressed that universal postal services to the extent of services and postal network density in Slovenia are comparatively high with other European countries. The scope of UPS in Slovenia has more than halved over the last ten years. By 2022 this volume will decrease by about 20%. In 2007, the Slovenian Post Office carried out 308 million of these services and in 2018 only 148 million more. In this area, however, Slovenia is no exception, as there are similar trends in other European countries, so in the coming years, the Slovenian Post Office will mainly plan new business due to the growing needs of the market for specialised tailored services, taking into account the latest trends in shipping of all kinds and at the same time improving delivery. It is noted that the Post of Slovenia has problems in the most profitable areas, this is in package delivery and urban centres, where today there is also strong global competition; however, the Post of Slovenia must cover the loss due to the provision of universal services while at the same time meeting the requirements of the Slovenian State Holding. In the face of increasingly acute issues of post office closure planning in the SMC, we propose that the Post Office of Slovenia establish contact with the local community and find a solution with it to the fullest extent that takes into account the reasonable needs of users of these services in the local environment, particularly in the rural area. It is necessary to ensure the appropriate level and accessibility of these services also in small and urban remote areas. There are some examples of good practice where the postal service contractor combined the tourist-information function with the post office and contributed even more to the local community with a new offer than the post office itself. However, it is up to the competent authorities to stem the trend of the collapse of the postal business network, while at the same time allowing the adopted solution of Post Slovenia to provide a financial sustainability of the universal postal service. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u12	Thank you very much, President. Good-bye, everybody! The current Postal Services Act clearly states: “The Republic of Slovenia shall, in the public interest, provide to all postal users in its entire territory under the same conditions a permanent, regular and smooth operation of the universal service, which may be interrupted only by force majeure or in situations dangerous to the supplier.” Universal postal service is provided by the Post Office of Slovenia, accessibility and price of universal postal service are determined by law. The postal service of Slovenia is obliged to provide this service in both places and at prices that are non-profitable. However, while the State on the one hand undertakes by law to provide public service, on the other hand, its 100 % ownership in the Post is considered to be a capital investment with which it must generate as much profits as possible. With this country Post Slovenia forces, for the first time, to close rural businesses, thereby reducing the accessibility of postal and other services provided by Post for the rural population; for the second time, this Post Slovenia forces the commercialisation of its activities. As a result, post offices turn into small stores offering food, drink, toys and so on, instead of providing postal services. Thirdly, the pressure of the highest return on capital at the high cost of universal postal services means saving on people, which is known on the salaries of workers and workers, saving on employment of new staff and consequently increasing the workload of employees; and writings, in addition to shipping mail, also transporting advertisements and the like. He used to have a letter bag, and today he has a motorcycle that has a front and back case for packages, letters, and commercials. And sometimes this is not enough, although the overall volume of letters sent over the last decade has decreased due to the internet and new technologies. The unsustainable situation has led to workers and employees of Post Slovenia, writers, to prepare a novel on the Postal Services Act and to collect more than 5,000 certified signatures so that they can submit it to the National Assembly. The law proposing that the budget country cover the loss generated by the provision of a universal postal service in the public interest of the State is not proposed by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia, is not proposed by the management of Post Slovenia, nor by any of the parliamentary parties. It was offered by workers and workers. The collection of signatures was organised by the Postal Workers' Union, which by the nature of the matter should not be concerned about the public interest and the profitability of the operations of Post Slovenia, but about the rights of workers and workers. In Levica, we support changes in the way we finance universal postal services. The current legal regime under which universal postal services should be financed from the so-called Compensation Fund is not operational. The establishment of the fund was decided by the governing policy in 2008, and today this fund is not a problem, nor is it a problem, since the Post Office of Slovenia would be practically the only contributor to this fund because of the share it occupies in the postal service market and would pay itself. Ensuring the appropriate legal basis for co-financing the implementation of the universal postal service, which is in the public interest of the State, is essential if we do not want the Post to create a loss due to the costs of implementing this public service. This is essential so that the Post Office of Slovenia is not under pressure to close down rural businesses after the greatest possible profitability. And this is essential for maintaining jobs in the Post Office of Slovenia, for decent salaries of workers and workers and for working under normal working conditions. The governing policy has rejected the proposal of the law, which they have proposed and have been tortured to collect signatures for it during the epidemic of workers and workers of Post Slovenia, at the Committee's hearing, saying that the Post must adapt to changes in the postal service market and introduce more sophisticated solutions. In this way, public interest has been placed on the market where iron competition laws rule and where the only interest is to forge as much profits as possible. It is a stepmother's relationship, with which the quality and accessibility of universal postal service is deteriorating over a long period of time. In particular, this destroys the Post Office of Slovenia, a company owned by a hundred percent of the country. The ruling policy, which has already concluded its consideration of the law on the committee and has prevented the National Assembly, as the country's highest representative body, from defining itself to the draft law tabled by the signatures of the electorate and electorate, has shown contempt for the people's initiative for the second time in one month. Again, it has shown in whose interest it actually works. Not in the public interest, even less in the interest of workers and workers, but in the interest of private capital, which will continue to serve well at the expense of the smaller competitiveness of Posta Slovenije, due to the burden it entails in the implementation of the universal postal service with which the public interest is exercised. And if you're wondering why packages of delay are coming these days and you have to wait in line in front of rare businesses that are still open, that's one of the reasons for this situation. We could've solved this with this marriage.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u14	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Dear colleagues and colleagues! The draft law on amendments and additions to the Postal Services Act – this is the Postal Workers' Union with 5,000 signatories – explains that the proposed legal solutions represent an adjustment to the rapidly changing situation in the postal services market in order to cover any net costs incurred in the implementation of the universal postal service from the State budget. In the view of the applicant, it is only in this way that a sustainable model of funding for universal postal services could be established, including receipt, guidance, transport and delivery of postal shipments, services recommended and value of postal mail, and the transfer of postal consignments to the blind and visually impaired. The Agency for Communications Networks and Services of the Republic of Slovenia shall issue a decision to the universal postal service provider on the basis of an analysis of the situation or a call for tender. Until 2023, he is a universal postal service provider throughout the territory of Slovenia. Where the obligation to perform a universal service entails net costs and entails an unfair financial burden, the operator shall be entitled to compensation for the provision of a universal postal service. The existing law currently provides that compensation shall be paid from the so-called compensation fund to which all universal postal service providers must contribute. The proposed law points out that the proportion of Post Slovenia within the provision of these services is 85%, with five or six smaller operators. This means that the Slovenian Post Office should pay almost all the funds from which its unfair financial burden should be borne by the Fund itself. This compensation fund has not justified its role to this day, and it is likely that another solution will have to be found here. In Novi Slovenija we are aware that the Slovenian Post Office, which is a 100 % company owned by the country, has recently become increasingly difficult to reach and has been forced to optimise the postal network, which unfortunately means closing non-profit mail, meaning that it no longer follows fully the objectives resulting from the European Directive requiring the maintenance of the rural postal network. Thus, in New Slovenia we wonder whether the proposed changes to the law would mean the end of closing down rural post offices. Unfortunately, the bill doesn't convince us to do that. The drafter cannot clearly identify the financial implications for the state budget, as this depends on the annual calculation of the net cost. Despite the fact that Slovenia’s post office has already prepared net cost calculations three times, ranging from 2016 to 2018, ranging from €17 million to €40 million; however, the Agency for Communications Networks and Services has repeatedly rejected a request for payment of the net cost. This means that, in accordance with the prescribed methodology, under the existing Postal Services Act, it has not been established so far that the Postal Service of Slovenia, by implementing the universal postal service obligation, generates a net cost as an unfair financial burden. Therefore, in Novi Slovenija, we did not support the draft law on the home working body. Thank you very much.

In the proposed amendments and additions to the Postal Services Act, something is disturbing, which at first sight may be less important detail. In the Desus Group, we believe that this is the whole matter of the relationship between Post Slovenia and the Agency for Communications Networks and Services. It is essentially in non-compliance with the methodology for calculating the net cost of the provision of universal postal services and on this basis the calculated unfair financial burden. At the same time, we must not ignore the fact that the Post is a capital company owned by a hundred percent of the state, which is subject to the provisions of the Company Act, so this is not a public undertaking, but a company which also provides public services. The most important question raised in the examination of the provisions of the draft law is whether the regulation of the way in which an unfair financial burden is paid from the State budget can mean the cessation of the closure of individual postal units. The answer is clear. This cannot be confirmed. So we're talking about universal postal service. Services are difficult to conceptualise because of their nature, mainly due to procedurality, by facilities that their operators, as well as by customers, and by all those who come in contact with them. Their characteristics are reflected in problems at all stages of their management processes and day-to-day implementation. In particular, delivery of letters is a type of service that is at the end of its life cycle and is increasingly needed by people using electronic communications. Proposalers of the novel need to be attached and recognised that the Republic of Slovenia has one of the highest quality in the implementation of the universal postal service, even though the price for the provision of these services is among the lowest in the European Union. Only Romania and Malta have lower standard letter prices. At the Committee on the Economy's meeting, we learned that the revenues from universal postal services account for 36% of the total revenue generated by the company in each business year. Mail as a universal postal service provider may submit an application or calculation, but according to law, the Agency for Communications Networks and Services is the only one responsible for determining the unfair financial burden. The calculation shall be made on the basis of the General Act on Accounting Information and the calculation of the net cost of universal postal service obligations. For 2019 the calculation has not yet been made and the Post Office has done so for the financial year 2018. At the time, the total revenue was EUR 234 million and the revenue from the universal postal service was EUR 84 million and EUR 300,000. The post office calculated the net cost as an unfair financial burden of EUR 36 million and EUR 900 000, 43.8 % of the total revenue from the management of the universal postal service. The calculation of the net cost of the obligation to perform this service shall be carried out in such a way as to distinguish between the net cost of the operator operating under the service obligation and the net cost of the same operator as it would have if it had operated without the obligation of that service. The postal service of Slovenia does not keep separate accounting records. Is it possible, then, to draw up an appropriate business simulation? The answer is in the palm of your hand — no. The debate on the proposed legislative changes was not about the net cost of unfair financial burden, but about the non-closure and closure of post offices. In the Desus Group, we believe that any payment of an unfair financial burden from the country's budget will not be able to stop the trend that has come into effect. The scope of classical postal business is decreasing because users meet their needs in different ways. We voted in favour of the proposed amendments to the law at the meeting of the mother's working body. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u42	The proposal for a law on the treatment of children and adolescents with emotional and behavioural problems and problems in education and education presents a systemic solution to a range of problems, schools and families; and, above all, to help children and adolescents with emotional and behavioural problems. Institutions for the education and education of children and adolescents with special needs for children with emotional and behavioural disabilities are under the responsibility of the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport. Their activities are also involved in the areas of social protection, justice and the specificities of the population, as well as health and police. The accommodation procedure shall be initiated in accordance with the law governing family relations or with the law governing the treatment of juvenile offenders. Children and adolescents shall be placed on the basis of a court decision, by way of a provisional order, by carrying out an emergency seizure by the Centre for Social Work, by deciding whether to take the child or to decide whether to place the child in a institution due to psychosocial problems or in accordance with the Penal Code and the Law on the Enforcement of Criminal Sanctions. For many years, individuals and institutions have pointed to problems in addressing and solving the problems of these children and adolescents. In 2015, according to the applicant, qualitative research was carried out on the subject of Slovenian development of educational institutions. It has highlighted substantive problems, including the fact that professionals in educational institutions face a number of difficulties in installing and treating children and adolescents when they do not have enough information about the child. In Slovenia, the network is not systematically connected, it is limited to professional government organisations, institutions for education and education, psychiatric departments, advisory centres, part of social work centres. The State establishes institutions for the education and education of children and adolescents with special needs for children with emotional and behavioural disabilities. In Slovenia there are nine such institutions that implement a training programme for children and adolescents. These are children who are at risk in the family, children who are at risk, and children and adolescents who are juvenile delinquents. The bill offers system regulation and four regional professional centres will be established. One of these will work alone and the other three will work in cooperation with other professional centres. The establishment and establishment of an expert centre will be governed by the competent minister. The centres are structured in a way that effectively, quickly and competently places a child or a youth by decision or decision of the court in which the most appropriate group of residences and education is based, among other things following the principle of the greatest benefit of the child or youth. A professional centre coordinating the cooperation of professional centres in a given area shall be responsible for deploying a child or adolescent to an expert centre within the area according to the type of assistance the child or adolescent needs. This will simplify the practice of accommodation and the professional and fair treatment of these children and adolescents. These professional centres will also provide professional support to kindergartens and schools working with children with these problems. Each of the four professional centres provides for a mobile team to respond to the request of kindergartens and schools. The law also sets out how to stay with treatment and in-depth monitoring of children and adolescents. Thus, the practice of living and education will be uniform. It is also stated that time can be spent in a professional centre, not in time, legally determined and limited to the number of years to be given to students involved in secondary school programmes. The participation of the relevant health services and the Education Institute is envisaged. The Centre for Social Work or the Court, which were the initiators of inclusion in the professional centre, check and monitor the stay and work of the child and youth in the centre itself. The law defines the return of a child or youth to the normal rhythm of life, the return of a home and further education, and also foresees a possible adjustment period that a youth may spend in a youth apartment. The proposer included in the production of the text a professional and also held a public debate and examined and answered in detail the proposals and questions raised, obtained the opinion of the Information Officer and the Ombudsman. We therefore support the law in the Slovenian Democratic Party.

On the other hand, this is a proposal for a law implementing Regulation (EU) of the European Parliament and of the Council on cooperation between national authorities responsible for implementing consumer protection legislation under the ordinary procedure. The draft law was tabled by the Government. In order to further explain the proposal, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary at the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology, Simon Zajc. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u44	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. Dear Members, good afternoon! The proposed law is needed in order to change the European Union's legal order in the field of consumer protection. It follows from the material to the proposal for a law that the main objective pursued by the proposal for a law is to adapt the internal legislation with the provisions of Regulation (EU) 2017/2394 and repeal Regulation (EC) No 1907/2006. 2006/2004. The Regulation is a procedural regulation applicable to infringements of 27 consumer protection directives and regulations, which are contained in various laws and regulations in the Slovenian legal order. This Regulation lays down the conditions for the cooperation and mutual coordination of national competent authorities with a view to the smooth functioning of the internal market and improving the protection of the economic interests of consumers. Infringements on the basis of which individual competent authorities will have to act are divided into infringements within the European Union, into widespread infringements where consumers are affected in at least two Member States, and into widespread infringements with the scope of the European Union where consumers are affected in at least two thirds of the population of the European Union. The main solutions to the draft law are proposed with a view to adapting national legislation to the provisions of the Regulation, namely, the mechanisms for the participation of competent authorities in cross-border dispute resolution; the powers of the competent authorities have been amended; the competent authorities and the single supervisory body for cross-border infringements are appointed; the conditions and means for effective and effective cooperation and coordination between competent authorities in cross-border infringements and the international coordination of competent authorities and the European Commission with a view to smooth functioning of the internal market and improved protection of the economic interests of consumers. And penalties shall be laid down in the event of infringements of the provisions of the Regulation. The single connecting body is the Ministry of Economic Development and Technology and the competent authorities are the Agency for Communications Networks and Services of the Republic of Slovenia, the Bank of Slovenia, the Infrastructure Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Agriculture, Forestry, Fisheries and Fisheries, the Public Agency of the Republic of Slovenia for Civil Aviation, the Public Agency of the Republic of Slovenia for Medicines and Medical Devices, the Market Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia and the Administration of the Republic of Slovenia for Maritime Affairs. Organisations that can inform the competent authorities of alleged infringements are the European Consumer Centre, NGOs established to protect consumer rights and carry out individual professional and research tasks in the field of consumer protection, the Chamber of Commerce and Crafts and other organisations and associations operating in the field related to the content of the infringement. The powers of the competent authorities are subject to a more detailed regulation of the powers laid down in the Regulation which the competent authorities do not yet have under our legislation and need to be re-organized in law in the Slovenian legal order. Most of the minimum investigative and enforcement powers provided for in the Regulation are already regulated in the Republic of Slovenia under the Inspection Control Act and therefore only the missing powers of the competent authorities are laid down in the draft law. These new powers, however, are the power to purchase goods or services, including covert identity, where appropriate, and to adopt measures related to websites. These are to order the company to remove the content; to order the provider of roaming services to remove, disable or restrict access to the web interface; to order the provider of exclusive transfer services to access the web interface or to direct display of the infringement warning on the web interface; to order the registries or domain registries, in appropriate cases, to temporarily delete the domain name system and to register a fully defined domain name, to enable the competent authority; to order the registrar or domain registrars to delete a fully defined domain name. Powers related to websites are extreme measures if there is no other effective option to achieve a cessation or prohibition of infringement and to prevent the risk of serious damage to the collective interests of consumers. The removal of content may be ordered by the authorised person of the competent authority. Other measures related to websites shall, however, be decided by the Administrative Court, on the proposal of the authorised person of the competent authority, on the basis of an infringement of the constitutional right to communication and the freedom of communication, the restriction of internet freedom, e-mail and freedom of expression. This Regulation shall be binding in its entirety and directly applicable in all Member States from 17 January 2020. The implementation of the Regulation ensures a greater degree of harmonisation of the actions of the competent authorities, which allow for more effective and effective cooperation between them. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u50	Vice President, colleagues and colleagues, Secretary of State, good afternoon! This year, a new European Regulation on cooperation between national authorities responsible for the enforcement of consumer protection legislation has entered into force, bringing better consumer protection. This Regulation ensures uniform respect for the legislation and protection of consumer rights within the European Union. The new rules will improve cooperation between national consumer protection authorities and the European Commission and help to tackle poor consumer practices more effectively. The enforcement of legislation will also increase the protection of consumers who cross-borderly purchase services and goods in and outside the online environment, reducing the risk of harm that consumers may suffer when purchasing goods abroad if they are not provided with this or when they do not receive all necessary information. The new regulation will also help to strengthen the competence of national consumer authorities, detect irregularities and implement measures to address them. Under the new rules, the Commission will also make it easier to alert national authorities and coordinate their actions to address the bad practices affecting most consumers in the European Union. Through a new online information exchange tool, consumer centres, consumers and trade organisations will be able to officially point out new market irregularities. The proposed law is therefore needed to change the European Union's legal order in the field of consumer protection. The new regulation of the European Union also requires Slovenia to appoint competent authorities to implement consumer protection legislation. These authorities are supposed to be the Market Inspectorate, the Infrastructure Inspectorate, the Agriculture, Forestry, Fisheries and Fisheries Inspectorate, the Agency for Communications Networks and Services, the Agency for Civil Aviation, the Agency for Medicines and Medical Devices, the Bank of Slovenia and the Administration of the Republic of Slovenia for Maritime Affairs. These authorities will also acquire new powers through the regulation to protect consumers; these are: apparent purchases of goods or services, including by secret identity, thereby checking financial flows to track unfair traders and online fraud; and actions related to websites. For example, an undertaking may be required to withdraw, cover or correct information containing fraud or fraud from a website or social media. In addition, the competent authorities will be able to impose fines and impose effective proportionate and dissuasive penalties in the event of infringements of the provisions of the Regulation. In this way, the new regime will strengthen consumer protection mainly online and increase transparency in online shopping. Consumers will therefore find it more difficult to be misled by different products presented as identical. Traders who continue to cheat will face high penalties. In the SMC Group, we are aware that the epidemic has affected many areas of consumer life, whether it is online fraud or cancelled travel arrangements. Therefore, in the SMC, we believe that there is a need to ensure, at national and European level, that we will also fight against consumer fraud in the future, together with the consumer protection cooperation network and in dialogue with platforms and all stakeholders. We are also on the consumer side when it comes to ensuring the protection of the rights of consumers or passengers in Slovenia and the European Union in the event of cancellation. In view of the many market developments and acute economic conditions that have lasted for several months, we are convinced that an analysis of the long-term impact of the epidemic should be prepared. The epidemic has also triggered a number of problems affecting the situation of consumers and will not be able to solve them without cooperation. It also showed that a digital transition and an appropriate adjustment to consumer policy are needed; this must be at the heart of the economic recovery plan. We are therefore convinced that in Slovenia we need effective consumer protection, which is a prerequisite for strengthening consumer confidence. Therefore, we will also unanimously support the proposal for a law implementing the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council on cooperation between national authorities responsible for the enforcement of consumer protection legislation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u58	Thank you so much for saying that. Nicely greeted! The bill you will now consider complements the Tonage Tax Act. The Tonage Tax Act introduces an alternative form of tax base for the payment of corporate income taxes, in the form of a flat rate. It therefore constitutes State aid for a certain segment of taxpayers. Companies operating on ships in international navigation may thus choose whether to pay a tax on profits on the basis of tax revenue and expenditure shall comply with the accounting rules or flat-rate on the basis of the net tonnage of the ship. Recognition and promotion of the importance of the maritime transport sector is in the interest of the European Union and its Member States. Maritime transport is also important for Slovenia. This importance was also recognised by the National Assembly, which adopted in 2006 the Resolution on Transport Policy of the Republic of Slovenia, in which the position was taken to ensure better conditions for maritime development and consequently an increase in the number of Slovenian seafarers. This will be done by applying the tonnage tax, as conditions for a competitive appearance on the world market for Slovenian taxpayers will be created, i.e. vessels and seafarers. As has already been pointed out in the introduction, the Tonage Tax Act is a state aid which must be in line with the applicable rules in this area, which is the applicable decision of the European Commission by which Slovenia was extended the scheme for the next ten years. The existing tonnage tax scheme expired on 31 December 2018 and Slovenia, or on its behalf, the Ministry of Finance, which is the holder of this scheme, has acceded to its extension. The European Commission extended the scheme for the next ten-year period until 31 December 2028. In doing so, Slovenia has undertaken to amend or supplement the existing tonnage tax law in order to ensure that the incomes of maritime transport activities are separated from those of the basic activity and income derived from the provision of ancillary maritime transport activities, in order to ensure that the treatment of dividends under the Tonage Tax Act is equal to that of the Member States of the European Union under the law governing the taxation of legal persons' incomes and that the treatment of the Member States of the European Economic Area is equivalent to that of the Member States of the European Union. This amendment further aligns the revenues from the ship's technical management services and the management of the ship's crew with the Commission Communication on the direction of State aid to ship management companies. That Commission Communication was adopted in 2009 and could therefore not be taken into account in the original tonnage tax law. In view of all the above, the Government proposes to support the proposal of the law before you. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u62	Dear Members and Members, distinguished Secretary of State! Today, for the second time in a period of one year, we are discussing the government's amendment to the Tonage Tax Act. Its key objective is to bring that tonnage tax in line with the European Commission decision extending the State aid scheme for maritime transport in the form of a tonnage tax to a new decade from 1 January 2019 to 31 December 2028. The amendments, if adopted, will apply to the calculation of the tax for the tax period starting on 1 January 2021. And why is it important for Slovenia to retain this tax or to comply with this State aid scheme? If this law were not adopted, Slovenia will be the only maritime country in the European Union that would not apply such a scheme or tax. This would result in the loss of our maritime industry's important competitive capabilities. The current shipowners would no longer be interested in the management of ships in Slovenia and in the registration of ships under the domestic flag, while foreign ships would not be included under the Slovenian flag in general. In addition, regardless of the primary tax nature of the law, this law facilitates the employment of Slovenian seafarers on these ships. As a result, this means preserving jobs and maritime knowledge. In the SMC, we have had a number of concerns in dealing with the previous novel. After further clarifications, we also supported the law in re-decision, but unfortunately it did not receive sufficient support. However, some of our early warnings are still valid today. In particular, it is a delay in the timely implementation of the approved scheme, since the law or tax on tonnage without an approved scheme of the European Union makes no sense at all. Even more so, even possible financial sanctions against the European Union are at stake without legal implementation due to possible illegal state aid. Indeed, this novelty brings substantively comparable solutions to those who were rejected in the first attempt, but the differences in Articles 14 and 15 of the Law are key. Article 14 contains a solution whereby, when it comes to a connected person with a shipper, the shipowner must also provide a traineeship position for seafarers of traineeships. This followed the expert public argument that regulation should have a positive impact on maritime education and be stimulating for later seafaring jobs. Article 15 provides for the validity and application of the Law on Tax Accounts from 1 January 2021 onwards, which means that the contested retroactivity is no longer applicable for 2019 and 2020. Finally, let me mention that at the Committee on Finance's meeting there was also a problem of tax control over the implementation of the law and the fact that both taxpayers who have applied this tax or this scheme so far have been taxable. We are aware of these issues, but we note that there are two completely separate issues. This law regulates only a specific tonnage tax and a related scheme approved by the European Union. The calculation, the assessment and recovery of the tax are subject to each individual tax procedure. Of course, we expect the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia to be able to recover these tax obligations effectively. This amendment to the law has nothing to do with this. All of the above in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre leads us to support this proposal of the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u66	Thank you, Madam Vice President. There's nothing I can't say that impresses me that you've noticed me. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The Tonage Tax Act was adopted for the first time in June 2007. State aid entered into force on the basis of the notification for a period of ten years, from 1 January 2008, when the European Commission first recognised such aid. The tax on tonnage is, in fact, an alternative form of determining the tax base for paying corporate tax. Legal persons operating on ships in international navigation may choose whether to pay a flat-rate tax under the tonnage tax scheme in relation to the net tonnage of the ship and the number of days of operation of the ship; or whether to pay a tax on the income of legal persons based on the determination of the tax revenue and expenditure in accordance with accounting standards. Other Member States of the European Union also use state aid in the form of tonnage tax. There is therefore less tax revenue in the budgets compared to corporate income tax, with the aim of promoting and developing maritime transport. Rejection of the law would mean that the legal regime that does not comply with the European Union acquis or the Community Guidelines on State aid will remain in force. State aid which does not comply with such a legal order must be recovered. Amendments to the Tonage Tax Act are needed for reasons of consistency with the regulation of state aid rules and the European Guidelines on State aid for maritime transport and the implementation is set for the future from 1 January 2021. At the moment, the taxpayers are liable to a ton of tax two, and the net impact on the revenue of the state budget is also projected to be very modest, only EUR 50,000 per year. In the Desus Group we estimate that state aid should be extended for a new ten-year period, as this is in favour of maintaining the status of Slovenia as a maritime state. One of the priorities of the Tonage Tax Act is to enable the employment of seafarers from Slovenia, where the planned targets have not been achieved in the past period. Therefore, the conditions for entry into the tonnage tax system for shipowners are supplemented by the provision that the activity of ship management is carried out from the Republic of Slovenia and that 51 % of the nationals of the Republic of Slovenia or of nationals of another Member State of the European Union are employed for the purposes of carrying out activities on land or ships. Countries also promote such employment through other elements of tax policy, such as reducing contributions and income. At the same time, it should be stressed that tax policy is only part of the policies that govern this area. In Slovenia, provision is made in Article 42 of the Income Act, which provides that the taxable base of the income achieved by the taxable person in connection with the operation of a long-distance commercial ship which sails the high seas, provided that the contract of employment provides that he is on board for a minimum period of 6 months or is absent from Slovenia for at least 6 months in the tax year, only 50 % of the income is included on board the ship. It is important to distinguish between revenues from maritime transport activities in international navigation on income derived from basic activities and on income derived from maritime ancillary activities. The proposal is logical because it is necessary to distinguish which income is eligible for favourable tax treatment under the tonnage tax system and which income must be taxed in the same way as that of all other companies in Slovenia. The outstanding tax liabilities of the debtors liable for the tonnage tax are within the competence and responsibility of the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia, which must be recovered without exception. The adoption of the law will provide a formal legal framework that can enable the operation of existing ships in Slovenia and possibly attract new business entities from this activity. In the Desus Group we will support the Proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Tonnage Tax Act. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u69	Thank you very much. The next Mr Jure Ferrjan will present the position of the SDS Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u70	Madam Vice President, thank you for your input. Dear Secretary of State, honourable Members, honourable Members! In 1991 Slovenia adopted the Resolution on the Maritime Direction of the Republic of Slovenia, which defines us as a maritime state. In accordance with the said resolution and the Community Guidelines on State aid for maritime transport, adopted by the European Commission in 2004, Slovenia introduced a tonnage tax in 2007. This tax introduced an alternative form of establishing the tax base for corporate income tax in the form of a flat rate for a particular segment of corporate tax taxpayers. Its purpose was and is to promote the competitiveness of European shipping, to preserve jobs and standards in the field of maritime transport, while preserving the unity of the European Union's internal market. The Tonage Tax Act also provides for a State aid scheme to be notified to the European Commission. The approved scheme under the original 2007 law was valid for a period of ten years. The law itself was then further amended and supplemented in 2009 to comply with the European Community decision. Taxpayers paid a million Euros per tonne tax between 2008 and 2019 on the basis of the Tonage Tax Act. If they were subject to an obligation under the Law on corporate income tax, their obligation would amount to EUR 24.5 million during the same period. The last entry into the State aid scheme under that Act was possible until 31 December 2018 and the Republic of Slovenia requested the European Commission in 2017 to extend the scheme which approved the extension, which means that the current scheme applies until 31 December 2028. The decision of the European Commission covers certain substantive areas differently than provided for in the applicable regulation of the Tonnage Tax Act. These areas should therefore be regulated in such a way as to comply with the European Union Decision or the applicable rules on State aid in maritime transport. This was also the condition of the European Commission when the state aid scheme was approved. The draft law therefore provides for the completion of the treatment of revenues from international shipping operations in order to separate revenues from maritime transport activities from those derived from the conduct of the basic maritime transport activity and the income derived from the operation of the maritime ancillary activity. The debtor must also maintain a separate bookkeeping for the income mentioned. The bill then provides that the operation of ships, which is one of the conditions for entry into the tonnage tax system, shall also be subject to the full management of the ship, which means the provision of technical management and management of the crew for the same ship. Equally, the tax treatment of income received from dividends under the Tonage Tax Act with the law governing the taxation of the income of legal persons is provided that the taxable person must increase the tax base, by an amount equal to 5 % of the amount of income thus received, in accordance with the law governing the corporate income tax. Equal treatment shall be given to the Member States of the European Union and the European Economic Area under the conditions relating to the ship and the flag requirements of the ship. The draft law provides for the application of tax accounts from 1 January 2021 onward, which means that the tax periods for 2019 and 2020 are not regulated. The proposal for a law is in line with the acquis of the European Union and the Community Guidelines on State aid and the Communication from the Commission on the direction of State aid to ship management companies. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will support the proposal for a law on amendments and additions to the Tonnage Tax Act.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u88	Thank you very much, Madam Vice President. Dear colleagues and distinguished colleagues, the representative of the government! Tourism is particularly important and extremely fast-growing economic for those countries with different natural characteristics. It is also an important factor in our lives from a social point of view as well as in terms of recreation and relaxation. The cable systems in our environment represent an important pillar of winter and annual mountain tourism. They are linked to development; they are a multiplier that brings added value not only in the narrow part where these systems are located, but much broader; therefore this economic logic of the cableway business is supported by both local communities and countries with different forms of aid and development resources. It is also true that the development process of mountain centres requires a high degree of mutual cooperation between different actors, particularly when it comes to defining objectives and related different forms and funding capabilities. Investments in cableway systems are linked to high fixed costs, fluctuations in revenue continue to make funding difficult and at the same time increase the level of risk, so in the Alpine countries, virtually everywhere in our area, there is some way that the state or the country is involved, regardless of who owns a particular cable system. The forms of integration are different. In certain cases, these are favourable low-interest loans, either in the form of single-investment subsidies or tax reliefs normally granted before the concession is granted. The proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on cableway installations for the transport of persons follows two objectives. Firstly, it is a matter of meeting the conditions for the technical suitability of cableway installations, linking the safety of users, operators and all others in the stages of production to operation required of us by the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of March 2016. Secondly, it provides a basis for the state and municipalities to allocate funds to co-finance the operation, maintenance and construction of cableway installations. The municipalities will be able to adapt co-financing according to the interest and financial capacity we have. It also seems important in the List of Marjan Šarca that the proposal for amendments and the amendment of the law does not require municipalities to carry out co-financing as an obligation, but rather as an opportunity for the co-financing process of operation, maintenance and construction to be carried out legitimately and legally. In particular, we see this as a step forward, so that mountain tourism and the related development of the other activities that accompany it will develop in the desired direction. We will support the Marjan Šarc Group for all of the above-mentioned proposals. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u90	Your Honor! The Law on Cable Devices for the Transport of Persons, which we are dealing with today, aligns Slovenia's legal order with European Union legislation on cable devices, while at the same time the amendment provides that the consideration of the draft law on the home body is of the highest interest. It provides a legal basis for co-financing the costs of operation, maintenance and construction of cableway installations by municipalities and the state. In this regard, it should be pointed out that only the possibility of co-financing is offered to the municipalities, but this is not their obligation if they do not decide to do so. These provisions were clearly clarified on the Committee by the representatives of the competent ministry and the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly. In the Modern Centre Party, this possibility of co-financing by municipalities or the state is welcomed, as we are aware that investments invested in cable infrastructure are only recovering from difficulties. We should also remember that Slovenian cableway operators have been in debt for many years, as they have been borrowing different loans for investments in recent years, due to green winters and the reduction in the number of skiers in recent years, and the repayment of these loans poses a major problem for them. We must be aware that the investments of cableway operators have a number of multiplier effects on the entire economy. According to some optimistic estimates, every euro invested in skis is expected to bring up to €6 in revenue, so it is only right that this financial burden is placed on other stakeholders; not that the whole burden is borne by cable operators themselves. Our skis are too small for owners to fund their own cable infrastructure. Investing in cableways is not only necessary because of the winter season, or mainly due to the summer season. Since cableway operators need a legal basis as soon as possible to finance their devices, we will support the proposal of the law in the Modern Centre. The implementation of this measure is necessary to mitigate the effects of the covida-19 pandemic. Three Members, Members of the Modern Centre Party, asked the Minister a question or initiative for a system-wide funding source; we also called on the Minister to examine the possibility of co-financing public transport with cableways as an economic public service chosen to act on the basis of public bus services. We must be aware that cableways are not only intended for skiers but are essential for access to leading destinations. Their operation is not only related to the winter season but also to the summer season or year-round season. In some cases, such cableways may allow transport connections to more difficult accessible tourist destinations. The current tourism development strategy also identifies six key policies and 75 measures to achieve the central objective of Slovenian tourism. These include legislative changes that will enable an enabling environment for the further development of tourism and the acceleration of investment in tourism infrastructure. The strategy proposes that at least part of the cableways become part of the public passenger transport system. On the basis of all the above, we will unanimously support the proposal of the Modern Centre Party Group. Thank you very much.

A clear indicator on the ground is also the garbage that illegal migrants leave behind. Last year only 10 tons of light waste had to be disposed of in the municipality of Illyrian Bistrica. This year, this number has already exceeded 11 tons. A healthy farmer's brain tells us that a small number of irregular migrants can no longer leave garbage behind. Therefore, as a member of the border and all colleagues in the New Slovenia Group, I am repeatedly asked by the National Assembly and the Government to draw attention to the pressing problem of not only illegal entry into the Republic of Slovenia, but also to the worrying movements of migrants against the interior of the country. Being at the border alone, I understand the plight of the burdened citizens of the Republic of Slovenia and their desire to resolve this problem as soon as possible. The inhabitants of the border are not afraid of our domestic Slovenian armies. On the contrary, her presence is desirable. Many border mayors have called for the activation of Article 37a to remind this esteemed assembly once again that 23 mayors of the initiative on the activation of this Article are signatories. The following was an open letter from 11 border Downland and Belarusian mayors on the need for priority security at the southern border and four other mayors who agree with the increased powers of the military on the basis of a positive opinion of experts. Thus, only five mayors have not joined the various initiatives on improving the protection of the Schengen border. In the New Slovenia Group, we therefore regret that, at the vote of the Defence Committee, the Proposal for a Decision on the exercise of the exceptional powers of the Slovenian Armed Forces in the wider protection of the state border has not been adopted again; and that is why, today, there is no vote in the Chamber of Democracy. However, this topic is not and must not be completed. We believe that the security of all citizens and citizens of the Republic of Slovenia is crucial, so we will continue to have a patient and reasoned dialogue with the parliamentary groups in the National Assembly. We hope for the reasonableness of our colleagues and colleagues in this Chamber, and above all we want to find a political solution that would have a unified agreement before, God forbid, an event that we will all regret. Then the competition in support of activation will be enormous and the content will be secondary. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u108	Thank you very much for your words, Madam Vice President. Dear Members, honourable Members! The Government of the Republic of Slovenia proposes to the National Assembly a draft law on the National Demographic Fund for legislative proceedings. The legal basis for this act was established in 2013 with the implementation of the Pension and Disability Insurance Act. This government is the first government to do so, which is proposing a law on the National Demographic Fund to the National Assembly for adoption. The demographic funds are state-owned investment funds, which are intended to provide pre-financing for pensions. As a result of demographic trends, most European pension funds generally raise less money than pension obligations. In Slovenia, too, the pensioner collects for a good 17% less annually than the pension expenditure. This missing funding replaces the state budget, approximately EUR billion in 2019. The purpose of the demographic fund is, therefore, to co-finance pensions in part of those transfer revenue from the State budget and thus to relieve the budget so that the budget can implement sound social, development, investment and other policies of the country. In view of the demographic trends in Slovenia, I am thinking mainly of population ageing, given the negative situation of income relative to pension fund expenditure and also given the current situation of high pressure to address the consequences of the covida-19 epidemic, we cannot talk about the total accumulation of assets of the demographic fund at the present time. This means that this demographic fund or its income would not be able to co-finance the pension fund until 15 or 20 years later. It is necessary to act fairly, in a balanced and economic manner already today, on the basis of general intergenerational solidarity, which will include both the younger and the older population of citizens and citizens of the Republic of Slovenia. The Government proposes to ensure that as late as possible the change in the generation curve or the prevention of this, and for the sustainability of co-financing of the pension fund by means of a bill, it proposes to ensure that the fund has as much equity and administrative consolidation as possible of capital investments, the accumulation of the fund's assets of 40 % of dividends and 60 % of purchases, the payment of 40 % of dividends and 40 % of potential purchases into the pension fund, the provision of funds to co-finance family policy measures of 10% of dividends and the provision of funds to co-finance policies for the care of older persons of 10% of dividends. In addition to this, a single, transparent, safer and more efficient corporate governance system of state-owned companies is established through the equity concentration of most of the capital investments of the countries. The management of these funds is based on the Fund's investment management strategy, which will also include investment policy and will be adopted by the National Assembly at the proposal of the Government. Companies owned by the Fund will have the required criteria for measuring performance and their operations. The independence, expertise, transparency, prudence and responsibility of the Fund’s investment management will be ensured by the Fund’s professional and balanced supervisory board. With the Demographic Fund Act, we will increase the security of pension payments and, of course, increase the amount of pensions. On the basis of the above, the Government considers that the proposal for a law is appropriate for further consideration and necessary to ensure the sustainability of pension funds and public finances and to co-finance family policies and care for the elderly on the basis of intergenerational solidarity. Thank you very much.
