ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u110	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! The purpose of the demographic fund is to ensure the long-term sustainability of the pension system by providing certain, further-funded funds for the purpose of paying pensions which will become part of the supporting pension scheme. This will achieve a more balanced income from the pension fund. When we are talking about the demographic challenges of a society, they must be understood in the broadest context and pervasive characteristics of all its causes and consequences. They are reflected in the composition, state and movement of the population. What can a Slovenian country that is the fastest growing society in Europe do to solve demographic problems? Slovenia is one of the smaller countries with a modern type of reproduction. This cannot give positive results in favour of an effective increase in fertility and thus the necessary balance between the working and retired generation. The requirements of demographic policy are therefore very strict, but most of all they are reflected in the stability and sustainability of the pension allowance, the adequacy of long-term care and the foreseeable situation of family policy. The social essence of the objective of the economic and social well-being of the people should be strengthened by implementing demographic policy measures, including through the use of available sources of funding resulting from the use of national wealth. The success and effectiveness of such behaviours depend on the political will to take certain measures. These measures have become much clearer than they have been in the mandate of this government so far. The establishment of the Demography Office, which will develop and implement a fundamental and development policy of governance with demographic challenges, is the first step towards more decisive action in the building of a future intergenerational, co-dependent society. The second step is to complete the establishment of a real demographic fund which, with clear objectives and corresponding financial inputs, could respond rapidly to the priorities that have been highlighted in the field of demographics in the Republic of Slovenia for many years. It is therefore time to decide on an appropriate institutional solution to regulate the establishment of sustainable sources of financing, the way in which and how to manage and manage, and the quality of the future operation of the umbrella manager of national wealth. The proposal for a national demographic fund law can, in the opinion of most Members of the Desus Group, realise expectations after the long-term stable co-financing of pension fund expenditure. Members of Parliament, Members, the capital company has been set up to cover the deficit in the pension and disability insurance system financed under the intergenerational exchange system. According to its content and objectives, since its inception it has acted as a demographic fund, formal transformation into a demographic fund means that it must be strengthened financially. Since 2000, the State budget has been legally responsible for balancing the revenue and expenditure of the Institute of Pension and Disability Insurance and for allowing it to operate in a balanced manner. The obligation of the State to cover the difference between the income and expenditure of the institution remains in the applicable pension law. The obligation to cover the deficit is not related to the specific rights of the public pension system, but rather to the undetermined and variable obligation of the State budget and therefore the apparent balanced budget of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office poses a latent threat to the balance of the state budget. The fact that the pension system is largely dependent on the state budget also contains an important implication in terms of the idea of the demographic fund. This is largely based on the sub-use of national wealth for the purpose of financing the pension system. On the income side of the Pension and Disability Insurance Office in the future, there will be no stable resources, both by content and by height. The revenue side with its own dynamics will not be able to follow the increasing cost needs. Under the provisions of the Slovenian State Holding Act, a capital company should be transformed into an independent autonomous demographic reserve fund by 31 December 2015. That didn't happen. During the previous term of office of the National Assembly, the Desus Group introduced a law on the Slovenian Demographic Reserve Fund into the legislative procedure. This happened on 30 January 2018. The government withdrew a month and a half later, so the legislative process was closed. The main reason for the inability to make decisions on this important institution is the dispersion between privatisation ambitions, the provision of a social state and the implementation of corporate governance. It is clear that learning from mistakes in Slovenian political and corporate practices means repeating them, not eliminating them. Corporate governance is an old blind spot of the market economy. Adam Smith is no longer able to find a good word in the wealth of the nations for equity societies, both those regulated by a special royal seal, like the others, completely private. Simplely, the capital managed by the administration is more foreign than theirs, and therefore, according to Smith's opinion, carelessness and loss. So politics has never been separated from stock companies, not yesterday and not today. The modern-day complexity of corporate governance has only increased by globalisation, financialisation and deregulation, and for the past 25 years there have been many reports of legislative changes, systemic recommendations, particularly the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development, and ethical codes. In the background, there is some disputed theory of companies and old market demagogies that the company has a key capital, administration and supervisory councils representing the interests of shareholders, not the interests of companies or a wider company. Sustainable development, social responsibility and economic democracy are the internal management problems of businesses in this respect. The business concept did not penetrate legislation, nor into business and accounting standards, so corporate governance is not only an economic but also a moral issue. We have regulated corporate governance according to the direction of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development. Its guidelines, which have a single objective: to prevent a country as owner from exercising its management and ownership rights as much as possible. Corporate governance has become part of the populist demagogies, the flawed exclusive sovereignty of capital and the divisional prey of miraculous human resources commissions. We've developed a system that obviously doesn't work. We're talking depoliticization, and then we're surprised when third-class careers are caught in human resources networks. We cannot demand the professional responsibility of the supervisory councils and have amateur governance, which is particularly the case with the dominant dual-tier system. Corporate governance must become a professional area with state certification. Loss of license due to infringements is a loss of profession. It's the only way to get serious and responsible people, not codes and courts. What was lacking to date in the proposals for the establishment of a demographic fund is additional to the level and structure of a stable resource. In recent years, Desus has taken care of one of the sources of funding. In the Slovenian State Holding Act of March 2014, we achieved that 10% of purchases are made from the sale of state assets for the financing of the demographic fund. To date, EUR 159 million has been raised in this account for the transfer to the Fund. The simulations of demographic movements do not predict anything optimistic. By 2050, according to Eurostat's projections, the proportion of people over 65 years of age from the current 20% to 31%, with fairly optimistic assumptions on migration. Currently, 1 out of every 5 inhabitants is 65 years old and over, almost 1 out of every 3 people will be living in 2050. On the other hand, the proportion between 20 and 64 years of age will decrease, further exacerbating the relationship between workers and those in retirement. The ageing of society means an inevitable increase in pressure on public resources to support health and the general economic well-being of older citizens. Demographic trends and the related issue of the fiscal sustainability of the pension system on the one hand and low pensions on the other are indicators calling for concrete measures to be taken by our pension system. And not only in the light of the tightening of the conditions of retirement and the overlap on the expenditure side of the pension fund, we have thus mitigated the need to increase public finances in Slovenia through pension reforms; now it is about providing additional financial resources for pensions. Ladies and gentlemen of Parliament! In the second half of the previous year, 2019, from August to November, as part of the previous coalition, we participated in the preparation of the Demographic Fund Act. There were two basic assumptions: the transfer of the Capital Society to the demographic fund and the 20-year accumulation period. In cooperation with the Ministry of Finance, five variants of the size and structure of the core capital were prepared. For each variant, future return simulations have been made taking into account the different rates of return and the accumulation period. Two major shortcomings have been identified. In any event, the size of the demographic fund was too low, as was its co-financing of the pension fund in the coming period. Since then, from November 2019 until today, another radical change has taken place. The epidemic has profoundly changed the state's public finances, as its consequences cannot be mitigated and limited without the use of fiscal policy instruments, without the use of urgent debt. This must be taken into account because it is an irrefutable reality. The different scenarios of time dynamics for both filling the demographic fund and drawing on its returns are thus placed in a completely new and unexpected context. Denial of reality means non-recognition of objective facts. In our case, this led to a revision of the position on the urgency of the accumulation period in the view of the need for the free accumulation of the assets of the future demographic fund and, at the same time, partial application of the benefits for the co-financing of the pension treasury. Future income, sales and dividend income will be used for the following purposes: 60% of purchases and 40% of dividends for non-accumulation; 40% of purchases and 40% of dividends for co-financing of pension funds; 10% of dividends for co-financing of policies taking care of older people and 10% of dividends for co-financing of family policies. In the Desus Group, we see the bill as a compromise between the current proposals, which have all experienced the same fate in order – whether they have not come into the legislative process or not. We estimate that good political action is precisely in the search for and making compromises, so we will support the National Demographic Fund Bill for the most part. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u117	Thank you very much. The position of the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party will be presented by mag. Marco Pogačnik. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u118	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Dear Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! We have before us the proposal for a law on the National Demographic Fund. The fact is that Slovenia needs a demographic fund, and the National Assembly has discussed it many times. We cannot go beyond the fact that the demographic fund should be established in Slovenia as early as 2012, this was foreseen by the Pension and Disability Insurance Act to be established in 2013. This attempt has failed. Article 79 of the Slovenian State Holding Act then provided for the transformation of the Capital Society into an autonomous demographic fund by the end of 2015 at the latest. Neither has this matter been realized and not carried out. Today we are in 2020, and before us is a new law, a new law prepared by the government of Janez Janša and a new coalition. In the Slovenian Democratic Party, we believe that this proposal is correct, and we are confident that it will meet all the conditions envisaged for a quality demographic fund. The proposed law aims to concentrate all the country's investments with certain specific exceptions, where ownership is to be maintained by the Republic of Slovenia, with one operator; the revenues from these investments are to be earmarked primarily for current and future generations of pensioners and for the implementation of family policy measures. The property of the Republic of Slovenia is currently owned by several legal persons who are pursuing various business objectives. The property is often not managed optimally, meaning that the profitability for owners is not maximum, but is subordinated to the different objectives of these companies. We have witnessed in the past that two state funds have voted differently in the societies' assemblies. With such management, one-stop-shop management could no longer be repeated in future such events. The main solutions to the law that we have on the table today are: the transformation of the Slovenian State Holding Company into a new company, that is, the National Demographic Fund, where ownership and management of the investments of the Republic of Slovenia are concentrated in one place; the transfer of all investments of the Republic of Slovenia with certain exceptions and investments of the Pension and Disability Insurance Institute in the Zavarovnica Triglav, d.d., and the Real Estate Pension and Disability Insurance Fund to the National Demographic Fund; the accession of the Capital Society of Pension and Disability Insurance and the Society for Consulting and Management, and the transfer of assets and rights and obligations of the Company for the management of bank receivables to the National Demographic Fund; the provision of the independence and autonomy of the National Demographic Fund for the management of investments; the determination of the content of the investment management strategy of the Fund, which replaces the State investment management strategy. The strategy is based on the classification of individual investment groups and thus on the identification of how to manage and dispose of investments. Demographic projections show a high increase in public financial expenditure for pensions and challenge all generations to agree together on how a country can ensure the most equitable and encouraging environment for co-operation by redistributing its revenues. For example, between 2016 and 2070 alone, expenditure from 21,9 % of gross domestic product to 28,2 % of gross domestic product should be increased. The number of pensioners is expected to increase by 20% by 2070. The Slovenian pension system, which operates on the principle of solidarity, is no longer able to ensure long-term sustainability in the situation of the smaller active population, as pension security contributions paid already do not provide sufficient funds for the payment of pensions; and the lack of resources must be provided by the State through the state budget. The main solutions of the National Demographic Fund Act relieve the pressure on public finances while providing additional funding for decent pensions. The importance of the demographic fund is also that it relieves the state budget not only for regular payments to the Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance of the Republic of Slovenia, but also during crises when the budget is even more burdensome. Under the proposed law, the management of the National Fund should be based on the principles of independence that ensure independence, independence and non-political action, the principles of due diligence and accountability, which ensure clearly defined rights, obligations, competences of individual holders, the principle of transparency in the decision-making process, the principle of economic efficiency, which must act economically, effectively and in a manner that allows the achievement of the objectives set out in investment management acts; the principle of security, dispersion, liquidity and return on investments that ensure the accumulation of resources for the co-financing of compulsory pension and disability insurance. The assets of the fund shall be accumulated permanently or indefinitely. The law provides that dividends from capital investments are used: 40 % for current payments to the Institute of Pension and Disability Insurance, 40 % for the accumulation of the assets of the fund, 10 % for the construction of dedicated real estate for the elderly, 10 % for family policy measures. The purchases from the sale of the domestic investments of the Fund shall be used: 40 % for payments to the ZPIZ and 60 % for the accumulation of the assets of the Fund. In the Parliamentary Group of the Slovenian Democratic Party, we will support the proposal of the law in the first reading. We are convinced that the bill is positive for all citizens and citizens of the Republic of Slovenia, for current pensioners and for all future pensioners. We cannot go beyond the fact that Slovenia needs a demographic fund. We have already mentioned in the introduction that it was envisaged that Slovenia's first demographic fund would be awarded in 2013, of which 7 years had passed. In doing so, at least three governments have been replaced and no government or coalition has been able to establish a demographic fund. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group, we are confident that this coalition will succeed, something that is good for all. Finally, we have our first reading of the law in the National Assembly. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, we commit ourselves to listen to all the proposals and to take all the good proposals into account in improving this law as well. We are convinced that this is a law that makes sense for both the opposition and the coalition to participate. Once again, the good proposals to be made will, of course, be taken into account.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u120	Thank you, Vice President. Representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues! From 2012 onwards, various proposals are being formulated to design a demographic fund in Slovenia. Opinions differ, but there are, above all, different views on ownership, governance, sources of finance, accumulation, policy and efficiency. At that time, five governments have been replaced, making it difficult for us to establish this fund, as politics has not yet been able to unify itself in this regard. In Slovenia, 20,000 young people enter the labour market every year and 30,000 people retire. The Slovenian labour market thus loses 10,000 workers each year. According to the projections of Umarje, which do not take into account migration, the number of workers in Slovenia will decrease by 150 000 in 2026 compared to 2006 and the number of people over 65 will increase by 180 000. That is why in the List of Marjana Šarca we are very concerned about negative demographic trends in Slovenia and we want to find an effective solution for both our age and the next generation. Unfortunately, however, the Government's proposal for a law on the national demographics fund ahead of us is by no means adequately designed to support it. In the List of Marjan Šarca, we advocate the idea that if the fund is established, it is not a transfer of ownership as envisaged by the government proposal, because we believe that the owner must still remain the Republic of Slovenia. In view of the government proposal, the fund would therefore not only be the manager, but also the owner of most of the country's capital investments, which would have been newly concentrated in this fund. This raises the question of whether, by transferring the ownership of state property to the demographic fund, the credit rating, the cost of financing, the deterioration of access to financial markets, the reduction of competitiveness, the level of productivity and the gross social product would be reduced. At the same time, there is also concern that this policy would allow for easier replacement of people who decide in supervisory boards in state societies, and thus more control over state assets, which we do not support in the Marjan Sarca List. The Commission also drew attention to these problems in order to prevent corruption, which has made extensive negative comments to the government's proposal and has cited the proposal, quote, "this is a shift from the proper perception and management of many years of recognized corruption risks in companies with the country's capital investment and does not contribute to enhancing integrity and preventing corruption". The government proposal does not take into account some of the very important provisions of the SDH law, particularly those which introduced safeguards to strengthen the independence of society in the functioning of SDH. At the same time, the Commission notes that the government proposal does not follow the principles of due diligence and accountability, independence and transparency, does not ensure greater accountability and integrity, and does not limit the risks of corruption and conflicts of interest, does not establish a framework for non-political action and thus does not provide for control over the assets of the members of the supervisory authority. The proposal also does not contain any prohibitions and restrictions on the Fund and its staff in the area of receiving gifts. In the List of Marjan Šarc, we believe that in order to avoid the collapse of the pension system in addition to a well-designed and well-thought-out proposal for a demographic fund law, of course with appropriate safeguards, it will also be necessary to consider complementary solutions that will provide additional resources in addition to the fund for co-financing pension funds. We will also actively seek solutions in order to regulate the long-term care system, increase productivity in Slovenia, focus on high-tech products and services, increase the share of active older people, especially in the sense that as few people as possible are in social support. The pension system is important for every society and for all generations, so it is necessary to start strengthening solidarity among generations and to find a way for young people to start believing that they will not only pay the system, but, after working hours, they will have decent pensions for a secure age. We believe that the country is the one that must encourage the second pension pillar, that young employees start saving for their pension or old age at the first salary and are not just waiting to receive a high pension from the country. For this reason, it seems very important for young people to learn about financial literacy in schools, to understand things better, to feel more actively involved in the pension system and to see a greater sense of saving for their age.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u124	Honorable representatives of the government! Dear Members and Members! At the October meeting, the National Assembly decided that the opposition proposal for a law on Slovenian demographic reserves was not suitable for further consideration. By completing its legislative path, it was possible for the National Assembly to begin its consideration of this government legislative proposal. In accordance with the ordinary legislative procedure, there will only be a general debate today, followed by two more detailed hearings at the Committee on Finance and perhaps a public presentation of opinions. In addition, the Act will also address the Economic and Social Council. All of this means that the solutions submitted are not definitive. And in the Modern Centre Party, we will insist that social dialogue is respected and that we also take into account all those proposals for solutions that will improve how state assets are managed. These are: concentrated management of strategic and important capital investments, compliance with the guidelines on good corporate governance practices and ensuring measures to strengthen integrity and accountability and to limit conflicts of interest, which we specifically requested in the SMC. In order to establish a demographic fund that will co-finance the growing needs of the pension fund, it is essential that we all agree. In Slovenia, every fourth Slovenian is over 65 years old and the pension system is far from sustainable. Unfortunately, pension security contributions paid do not provide sufficient funds to pay pensions; therefore, the lack of funds from the budget must be provided by the State. Following the example of other countries, the Republic of Slovenia also decided to set up its demographic reserve fund when adopting the 2012 transition pension reform and to establish a legal basis for its creation. In the mandate of the Cerara government, we have unfortunately failed in our efforts to harmonise the proposal for a law on the demographic fund. We have also worked constructively in the government under the leadership of Marjan Šarc to form a legal solution for the establishment of a demographic fund. He was prepared to some extent for a harmonised legislative proposal, but disagreements reoccurred between the parties of the then coalition, which prevented further consideration. We hope that this time the third attempt will be more successful in eight years, especially because the demographic funds in the Member States of the European Union are not new or rare elsewhere in the world. Demographic funds are a type of state-owned investment fund. They are set up to provide pre-financing at the pension fund during the period when its expenditure will be higher than its revenue. It is therefore not uncommon for the demographic fund, as proposed by this government, to be supported by the leading instigator of the so-called Constitutional Lok Coalition, economist Dr Jože P. Damian. And he sees it as meaningful and useful. In the Modern Centre Party, we will be committed within this government concept to transform this so-called reserve pension fund into a pure form of the state wealth fund. It is clear from the expectations and proposals of coalition partners that the Fund should also provide funds for family policy measures, the building of real estate for the elderly and the like. Also in the Modern Centre Party, we are aware that demographic policy is much broader and must address comprehensive areas, including long-term care, housing policy, youth policy, intergenerational cooperation, and policies that address brain drains related to education, job creation and youth and older mobility. Therefore, in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, we will support the conclusion that this law is suitable for further consideration. In this place, however, we are also alert to our expectations and warnings. Since, normally, national wealth funds in other countries have additional sources of funding in addition to the primary source, it would be reasonable, in our view, to seek these additional resources and to enable the Fund to meet its mission more easily. We would also like operators with international experience to be acquired to manage the assets of the Fund. However, this will only be possible if the Fund has an appropriate remuneration policy. Once again, we wish to recall that the establishment of the National Demographic Fund must not be an excuse for not reforming our pension system in a timely manner. The fund is not a magic wand that will fully solve the pension problem. And a new pension reform will be needed sooner or later, as the White Paper on pensions also points out. We stress that the role of the reserve demographic fund can only be attributable to the fact that the pension scheme is partially and to a lesser extent burdensome by covering the difference between income and pension fund expenditure. Thank you very much.

The government of Janez Janša by establishing a demographic fund does not save pensioners and families, but saves itself. It will ensure centralisation of control over ownership and management of as much as €8.6 billion of state assets through the concentration of state property. The government has turned on the green light to privatise the 49% share of Slovenske železnice – Freight Transport, which is one of the most profitable companies in the Slovenian Railway Group. The handover of half of EP Holding’s share will not serve the strategic interests of the Slovenian economy, but rather the private interests of the multinational corporation, which owns around €12 billion in funds and investments and which, in recent years, with the acquisition of former public thermal power plants and transport infrastructures in France, Poland and Slovakia, has accelerated the construction of a private energy and logistics empire that stretches across Central Europe. In addition, the Janševa government has taken the initiative to conclude an agreement between Slovenia and Hungary on the construction and management of the second track of the Divač-Koper railway. Because of the damaging decisions of the government of Janez Janša, one of the most important and expensive infrastructure projects in the country will thus become the hostage of the political and capital interests of Hungarian Prime Minister Viktor Orban; the hostage of the associated economic elites. With the arrival of Orban's money, which we don't need at all, the project will cost 300 million euros more and Hungary will also be counting on a high profit of 2TKK. This profit will be provided by Slovenia with public taxes to be paid: Luka Koper, Slovenian Railways and our carriers. It will also have to contribute to the state budget, while Hungary will not – except for the initial investment – contribute anything. However, for 35 years she will co-owner of critical Slovenian infrastructure and thus gain a veto on all important decisions of 2TKK; in addition, she will receive another 30,000 square metres of land owned by Luke Koper. There is no way that such a government can trust that it will operate economically or in the public interest with EUR 8.6 billion of state property. More than 240 000 pensioners will still receive pensions below 600 euros. The fact is that the Fund’s public financial effects on the pension fund will be completely negligible. The Institute for Pension and Disability Insurance has regularly grown around a billion euros in deficits in recent years. Last year, the budget in the ZPIZ paid 965 million euros and this year's budget earmarked 1.15 billion for pensions. There is virtually no funding for long-term care currently in the budget, although at least EUR 300 million would be required for the system regime at annual level. We spend around €600 million of budget resources on family benefits and parental allowances at annual level. Together, this is about 2 billion budget commitments, and in 20 years this amount will double at least – to EUR 4 billion or more. All these obligations could not be covered by any domestic demographic fund. Slovenia is not Norway, which has the largest public pension fund in the world with more than EUR 900 billion of assets and can count on excessive profits in oil production. Even the problems mentioned above will not be solved by the demographic fund as envisioned by the current government. The government law does not provide for any accumulation period that would allow the accumulation and nobility of long-term assets, but provides for the immediate regular payment of funds for the purposes of family policy, long-term care and pensions: 40% of dividends and purchases paid to cover the obligations of ZPIZ, 10% of dividends for the construction of homes for the elderly and 10% of dividends for the financing of family policy. In the event of realistic projections, in 2040 the Fund would be able to pay around EUR 200 million to the pension fund, which is only around 8% of the expected deficit of the STIZ. The projections of Majcna and Sambta show that in 2040 the pension fund deficit will amount to EUR 2.4 billion. If it were the estimated 200 million euros that the fund would have paid into the pension fund in 20 years, in fact, for the additional net income of the pension fund, it would have dealt with a negligible amount, but that is not the case. The more state property or income we allocate to the fund, the less revenue the state budget will have, and just as little will be the budget obligations to cover the HIPC deficit. Basically, it's a neutral accounting operation or a transfer of money from one pocket to another. The budget will be waived by state capital investment dividends and left to the fund so that the fund can then relieve the budget with these funds. The Fund may be able to obtain additional funds from portfolio investments in global financial markets, but this will only be a sea lung when compared to the expected pension fund deficits. But as it is said, the key issue is not the impact of the demographic fund on the pension fund, but the impact of the fund on the ownership structure and management of the country's capital investments. In this regard, the government proposal does not bring us anything good, just new levers for the damaging privatisation of state property. The government law mentions only the division into strategic investments and non-strategic or portfolio investments with which the fund can freely dispose; however, there are no significant investments in which the state must maintain a 25 % plus 1 share under the current capital investment management strategy. Therefore, there is a reasonable risk that the current important investments will also deteriorate to portfolio level, which would mean that the fund would have a completely free hand in privatising companies such as Krka and Petrol. In addition, there is a provision in the law that allows the fund to reduce its share of strategic investments below 50% and to leave the pursuit of strategic objectives to concessionaires. In addition, they will be given new arguments for privatisation. Since the law provides that 40% of the purchase from the sale of state property is intended for STIZ, the government will always be able to say that privatisation is in the interest of pensioners because it provides additional funds for pension funds. All of these are not guidelines for management, but for the sale of public property. In addition, the demographic fund is designed to pursue profitability motives, strategic objectives and to take care of pensions and other social policies at the same time. These motives, however, are soon to come into conflict. This point is well illustrated by the example of Post Slovenia. Instead of providing postal services in the public interest to all citizens as a state-owned company, the Slovenian Post Office, on behalf of rationalising and hunting the profitability targets of the country's annual capital investment management plans, closed post offices across Slovenia, thus creating an even greater burden on employees. Due to the imposition of profitability criteria, the quality of postal services today is at the bottom. Post workers and workers are caught in a vicious cycle of low wages, raising standards, performing many overtimees and working in totally intolerable conditions. Last but not least, the legal determination of the composition of the organs is significant. The Assembly, as the main decision-making body of the demographic fund, is fully aligned with the government. According to Janša's Government, it is only by itself that it can manage state property, which is owned by all citizens. In addition, the government proposal simply overruns Lahovnik's law, which limits the remuneration of senior staff in state-owned companies, as well as the Law on the participation of workers in governance. The government of Janez Janša will thus have full control over virtually all state assets, and the leading staff of the demographic fund will be able to reward them for their service with unsavoury high wages. All those who have the greatest credit for the production of public wealth by the Janša government, i.e. workers, will be completely excluded from the administration of state property. Neither pensioners nor workers nor other citizens need a demographic fund, as Janša’s government had envisioned. In order to consolidate its power and control over state property, Janša alone needs it. We do not need Janša's government at all, so we will strongly oppose its proposal for a National Demographic Fund Act. Thank you very much.

The only assessment of participants is that despite the merger of all four state-owned investment managers under one roof, the demographic fund will not be able to cover the pension gap and that most of the inflows will be spent on current pensions and for future generations there will be little fighting. With €8.6 billion of combined state investment, most of which are strategic, it will not be possible to achieve the stated objectives, namely to provide pensions and additional funds for other demographic objectives, family objectives and long-term care. That's not possible. It's just a smoke screen, because it's gonna be hard to do something to finance pensions, according to the vice president of the Association of Managers. In the Chamber of Economics, they warned that it was not some kind of fresh means at all, since they had so far expired into the budget. They estimated that this fund could create around €280 million of inflows per year at best, which means that they could only follow the reconciliation of pensions and that without sale, future challenges could not be addressed. At the conference, the economic profession considered that the proposed solutions to the law with the demographic fund had nothing to do with it. The existing assets are too small and if we sell them, the fund will eventually end itself. The management solutions of the law were also subject to serious criticism. These abolish staff procedures in the selection of fund supervisors, reduce the criteria for this function and do not require non-politicality, which is in direct contradiction with the OECD recommendations, eliminate all security guards so far when appointing supervisors in state companies, as well as a chapter on integrity, which means that anyone can be appointed to the Supervisory Board. Corporate governance has deteriorated significantly. The OECD representative present, as we are also a member of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development, Mr Hans Christiansen, said that the OECD members, who have established good practices in corporate governance of public services in line with the OECD guidelines, have important benefits. In doing so, he stressed that state companies are owned by citizens and not owned by ministers or government. Governance should therefore be in the public interest. He also said that poor governance of state-owned enterprises is not a reason for privatisation but can be improved. SDH, according to its results, which is the countries of these guidelines, is a good example for this. He pointed out that the problem is that state-owned companies are often not sufficiently separate from political influences, as members of the supervisory committees and administrations are still not sufficiently independent in their decision-making. The worst cases are where state companies are only a extension of power. And that's what we seem to want to do with this bill. The anti-corruption commission also opposes legal solutions because it poses high corruption risks. Representatives of trade unions, members of the Economic and Social Council also assess that the proposal is flawed and is not in line with the ESF. The law is neither a day nor a public debate, which is in fact unacceptable for such a law. In all of this, it seems that the proposal for the demographic fund ahead of us is just a smoke screen, as expressed at the conference, for political purposes. Therefore, this proposal does not appear appropriate for further discussion. These are the positions of the profession. Thank you.

Injecting a certain amount of water with additives is an essential component of oil and natural gas production, as well as drilling of wells. The deep drilling of wells, including wells drilled for geological and geomechanical research, for example, for construction purposes, is carried out by a flush that lifts drilled particles and maintains the stability of the well. Concession acts or regulations governing mining rights shall, in a specific case, be subject to the Regulation on the granting of mining rights to holders of mining licences for exploration or extraction of mineral raw materials issued on the basis of laws and are no longer applicable and cannot be amended. The text of the bill is not adequately formulated and does not take into account the financial consequences of interference with acquired rights. Only one concession contract for the exploitation of oil and natural gas is issued in the Republic of Slovenia. For the use of hydrocarbons by injecting water into an environment where an environmental impact assessment is required, it is necessary to carry out an environmental impact assessment for these parts. This refers to the approach to risk management in the sense that a policy or measure should not be implemented if there is a possibility that it could cause harm to the public or the environment and if there is no scientific consensus on the issue at hand. As regards only the method of hydraulic fracturing, it should be clarified that we know two basic ways which differ substantially and differ mainly in scope. Thus, we know the low volume or conventional, which is and could continue to be carried out in Petišovci, and we know the high volume or non-conventional, which is being implemented or used in the United States. And these two matters, these two methodological forms or methods of fracturing, must not be equated. In Slovenia this conventional hydraulic fracture has already been carried out in the past, with more than ten times since 1956. Technologically speaking, modern fracturing is really nothing new, and so far fractures have had no known negative effects on the environment since 1956. The implementation of this unconventional American hydraulic fracture in Petišovci is not even possible. The Geological Survey of Slovenia, not anyone, not me, not any other party, but the Geological Survey of Slovenia explains that in the case of non-conventional fractures, that is, this American, there would be a rapid oversaturation with water spilling laterally into the hydrocarbon layer. This may or may not seem like some kind of scientific fiction to anyone or even to the proponents, but these are simple facts that have not yet been presented to the public here. And there seems to be another important thing in this regard. The Geological Survey of Slovenia explains that the conventional way is not known to have negative effects on the environment, i.e. this non-American way of fracturing. This explains the Geological Survey of Slovenia. Further, when we talk about where the so-called fracking is banned. At the time of the previous hearing, a colleague from Levice claimed that, among other things, fracking was prohibited in Croatia and Germany. These claims are at least incorrect and misleading. Non-conventional fractures are prohibited in Croatia and are still allowed conventionally. As regards Germany, which has also been mentioned today, conventional gas production is not prohibited. Prohibited, and only temporarily, is the non-conventional extraction of gas. And as far as the non-conventional method of gas production is concerned, it is even allowed for research purposes. And just as the proponents do not want to separate between these two ways, the distinction between conventional and non-conventional fracturing is seen, unfortunately, only in rare media, and this is in fact essential for understanding the use of hydraulic fractionation. This will eventually be confirmed by any expert who is professionally involved. Fear of fracking polluting drinking water has also been voiced several times. In the case of Petišovec, it is necessary to make it clear that the effects on water are unlikely. Drinking water is drawn from aquifers about 20 meters deep, and quality drinking water is also found in aquifers up to 200 meters deep, and finally thermal water reaches the deepest to thousands of meters, and current plans in Petišovci predict the fractionation of gaseous layers from the depth of 2 thousand 700 meters down, that is, 2 thousand 700 meters down. The question that we have to ask ourselves today is whether we are going to stem from professional grounds in the legal regulation of hydraulic fractures or whether we are going to come up with the kind of arguments that the profession has rejected long ago. And the fact is that the prohibition of hydraulic fracturing is first and foremost a political decision. In this respect, the profession has already taken its position, but unfortunately, this has never come to the fore when all proposals for a ban on fracking are being addressed. If we really want to make a decision that will be in everyone's interest, then everyone must step back and take a solution that will, above all, be a compromise. Thus, in our view, in the National Assembly, we should primarily discuss the extent to which we wish to prohibit fractures, and the question of whether they are also prohibited for research purposes is also important. Are there any guidance on planning, design, implementation and control of projects in which hydraulic fracturing is carried out? What about environmental monitoring? Could this be a source for a national demographic fund, in the light of Norway’s example, in view of the topic we had before with regard to the national demographic fund? All of this and many others are questions to which we should look primarily for answers in the types of experts. And only at the moment when we have these answers could we make some deliberate political decision. Thank you very much.

The consequences of fracking can also be seen in a number of cases from abroad. There have been more frequent and more severe earthquakes, pollution of groundwater and drinking water. Fracking can also affect air quality. Many countries have already recognised the harmfulness of this method and have also banned it legally. Among them are Britain, Ireland, France and Bulgaria. The proposal also follows the commitments of the Paris Climate Agreement regarding the abandonment, acquisition and use of fossil fuels. An exception to the faction of our territory is the British corporation. Although the Agency of the Republic of Slovenia has decided that an environmental impact assessment and environmental consensus must first be carried out in order to draw on the gas by means of hydraulic fracturing. This has also been confirmed by the court. Therefore, Slovenia received threats from the said corporation by way of an action and procedures for the initiation of an arbitral action were even initiated in early October. This is therefore a kind of absurdity, as the country has legally implemented regulations on the protection of the environment and human health. In doing so, it can be understood that the corporate behaviour does not wish to proceed. I repeat, however, that the behaviour of the corporation can be understood that it does not wish to proceed with the procedure, and therefore it would be only logical that the ARSO, which runs this procedure, should also complete it. In the SAB Group, we believe that Slovenia should also follow the practice of countries that have already recognised and consequently banned fracking. This will ensure the health of people, their property and the environment and the unspoiled nature. On the previous proposal, the government was expected to give a negative opinion, as they felt the proposal was not adequately formulated and did not take into account the financial implications. In the SAB Group, we disagree with the Government and assess that the proposal is suitable for further consideration, but at the same time we call on the Government or the authorities in the body to complete the ongoing procedures and to eliminate all these doubts and possibilities for redress. ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u238	Mr President, thank you very much for your words. Dear Members and Members! Before you today is the proposed amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act, the new CAP-O. The basic objective of the bill is to ensure procedural conditions for the functioning of the European Public Prosecutor's Office, which the European Union regulation imposes on us. The European Public Prosecutor will indeed be responsible for the prosecution of criminal offences that are detrimental to the financial interests of the European Union and other related criminal offences. Our arrangement, which includes a judicial investigation, needs to be adjusted by means of a so-called prosecution investigation. The new solutions are without prejudice to the basic assumptions of the regulation of our criminal procedure. Another comprehensive set of solutions is changes relating to the situation and rights of juvenile delinquents involved in the implementation of the European Union Directive. In addition, we are implementing a new constitutional case-law and are proposing solutions that follow the effectiveness of criminal treatment, the cost-effectiveness of procedures and the greater transparency of the functioning of justice. Let me put them briefly: a combination of cases is also introduced at the prosecution level in the pre-trial procedure; a preliminary knowledge is provided of qualified prosecutors with essential grounds for rejecting criminal charges for the most serious crimes; a requirement is made for due diligence in the labelling of evidence; the duty of the appellant is introduced to carry out the main proceedings; the possibility of giving separate opinions to the Supreme Court in deciding on a request for the protection of legality in criminal matters is introduced; the possibility of videoconferencing the execution of orders is re-defined; the time limits for the first action of the Public Prosecutor following the receipt of a complaint against a known offender are laid down. The Committee on Justice has also adopted an amendment allowing access to information of a public nature in criminal matters under the Public Information Access Act. The opposition proposes the deletion of this solution, and a day ago, the Information Commissioner also addressed you with an opinion explaining extensively that long-standing regulation and practice are maintained through a new solution. With the opinion of the Information Commissioner, we agree at the Ministry and, of course, I also suggest that you reject the opposition amendment. If business conditions are met, I propose that the third consideration of the bill be carried out at this meeting. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u240	Thank you for your word. The Committee on Justice considered the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Criminal Procedure Act to be the parent working body at its 21st session 2. 12. 2020. Representative of the proposed Minister of Justice, Mag. Ms Kozlovich presented the main solutions to the bill in the supplementary explanation of the articles. She pointed out that she wanted to ensure, in particular, compliance with the European legal order and to ensure procedural conditions for the functioning of the European Public Prosecutor's Office, as well as special procedural guarantees in criminal proceedings for juvenile suspects or accused persons. The bill seeks to achieve a more rational regulation and a smooth course of criminal proceedings, to remedy problems in practice, to simplify criminal proceedings with civil justice and to improve the current regime with certain minor or editorial changes. In a presentation of a written legal opinion, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service pointed out that the amendment to the law represented extensive and highly diversified content that was poorly transparent and non-systematic, indicating the need for a comprehensive reform of the law. It further raised concerns about the envisaged introduction of separate opinions by Supreme Courts in criminal matters with regard to the establishment of a deadline for the conduct of the Public Prosecutor's Office following the receipt of the complaint and the mandatory consultation of the State Prosecutor with qualified prosecutors prior to the rejection of the complaint. An additional oral opinion was given on the amendment to Article 14, which introduces in the law a new substance concerning access to data from individual criminal cases, which means, in the opinion of the Legal and Legal Service, an infringement into the entire Criminal Procedure Act, by applying new terms, further confusion in particular as regards the relationship between the Criminal Procedure Act and the Public Information Access Act and is not adequately explained and explained. In view of the purpose of the criminal procedure, it is therefore questionable whether the application of the Public Information Access Act would provide sufficient protection for both the judicial work and the forecasting part of the proceedings in order to adequately protect the fundamental human rights and freedoms of the individual protected in these proceedings. Attorney-General Drago Šketa pointed out that the Act under consideration is already the 15th Amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act, which gives new powers to state prosecutors, with the State Prosecutor's Office operating in a refractory structure for a long time. The Supreme Public Prosecutor welcomes the majority of the proposed amendments and joins the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service on the amendment to Article 14. In their view, anyone, after all, will be able to obtain data from a criminal record under the Public Information Access Act, thereby eliminating the confidentiality of the criminal procedure in its entirety and affecting the conduct of the criminal procedure is taken from the judicial sphere and subordinated to another State authority. He also drew attention to the proposed deadlines for the conduct of the Public Prosecutor following the receipt of the complaint and to the proposed mandatory consultation of the Public Prosecutor with qualified prosecutors prior to the rejection of the complaint. The Committee was also represented by a representative of the State Council, the President of the State Prosecutor's Council and a representative of the Chamber of Lawyers of Slovenia. In the debate, opposition Members stressed that such an important law expected a coordinated, professional cooperation between the applicant and the professional public. However, it was possible to conclude from a number of comments that the proposed amendments to the Act were largely not harmonised. In particular, they opposed the amendment to Article 14, which, with disproportionate amendment and expansion of access to information in the judicial file, denies the concept of the Criminal Procedure Act. They also tabled a proposal for an amendment to the Committee, which was not adopted. The coalition Members supported the proposed amendments to the law in their debate. The Committee, by adopting all the amendments tabled and one of its amendments and all the articles, has jointly drafted the text of the amended law in which amendments have been adopted and is an integral part of the report of the Committee.

ParlaMint-SI_2020-12-15-SDZ8-Izredna-53.ana.u256	Thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! Amendments to the Criminal Procedure Act are primarily necessary in order to comply with the Regulation on the implementation of enhanced cooperation with regard to the establishment of the European Public Prosecutor's Office. In addition, the Directive on procedural guarantees for children suspected or accused in criminal proceedings is being implemented. The provisions of the law shall be supplemented by providing these persons with special procedural guarantees. As a result of the decision of the Constitutional Court, the notification of the names of the parties to the Court of Appeal shall also be governed and the time limit for the application for the replacement of the prison sentence by house arrest shall be extended. All of these are changes and additions to the CAP, which are not disputed and supported by the SAB. However, we will not support the bill for two reasons. The first, in the form of a less important reason, is to introduce a separate opinion of the Supreme Judges when deciding on a requirement for the protection of legality. Although it may be a minor amendment to the Criminal Procedure Act, in the SAB, we have already pointed out in the previous examination of the Criminal Procedure Act that such an arrangement can have an extremely negative impact on people's confidence in the legal functioning of the judiciary and on people's confidence in the rule of law. The same thing was pointed out at the time by the Legislative and Legal Service, and this same provision was deleted from the law at the time, and at the last hearing on the Committee, despite all the warnings made by the shareholders, this same article was adopted. I am aware that a separate opinion can have a great deal of added value when it comes to a separate opinion at the European Court of Human Rights or, say, at the Constitutional Court or in a fair trial. Every time we talk about civil procedures, we're sure. In criminal proceedings, however, there is a statutory or Senate trial and a way of determining uniform case law through a general meeting of the Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia. The introduction of separate opinions in criminal proceedings will constitute an exception to the current Senate decision-making regime. The Legislative and Legal Service has pointed out that the introduction of separate opinions by the CAP on the basis of a civil procedure or the Constitutional Court raises legitimate doubts. In particular, it would be inconceivable for the Supreme Court to rule on a judicial judgment when an extraordinary remedy is being examined. For example, the Court would confirm such a judgment, not only at the first stage but even at the second stage, this judgment would be legally binding and then, when deciding on an exceptional remedy, a separate opinion would be given by one of the judges. So one of the judges would have otherwise explained the conviction. And this judgment, which is subject to the highest evidentiary standard in criminal proceedings. In our country, where people already question the fairness of the trial and the independence of the judiciary, can such separate opinions in criminal proceedings help to increase confidence in the work of the courts? In SAB, we don't think so. Another matter that has disturbed us more significantly is once again the subjugation that the coalition has afforded in this law. In dealing with such an important law as the Criminal Procedure Act, the system law, there was suddenly an amendment before Members, which essentially denies one of the provisions of the Criminal Procedure Act. Article 14 of the bill requesting the coalition to give everyone, including third parties, access to information contained in criminal records. I would like to point out that today's seductions continue. We are not talking about the public of judgments or the public of the work of the courts, about how much they are doing, what they are doing, where individual proceedings are taking place, no, we are talking about the access of third parties to the content of the criminal record. In doing so, the coalition falsely relies on the Supreme Court judgment of May this year and interprets it as if this judgment relates to judicial decisions. The decision that I have before me and can be looked at by anyone who wants it is by no means a provision. All this judgment provides is that, in the case of a criminal record being examined during criminal proceedings, a judge shall determine whether or not an inspection is permitted, and not someone else. In fact, this provision has been overtaken in the coalition and has now been allowed to intervene in criminal proceedings before the case is legal, irrespective of the domain of innocence, irrespective of the protection of the dignity of the individual, irrespective of the fact that a criminal procedure can be contaminated. Against this, both the Supreme Court and the Prosecutor's Office, both the Prosecutor's Council and the Bar have been warned. There's nothing left. Once again, a coalition with a harmful amendment overpowered the system's system and threatened a very functioning system to look into the criminal file. We will not support this marriage in the SAB.
